### Noise floor — same-GL repeats

Runs: GL 1 × 2. 190 scenario × version pair(s) measured at least twice (GL 1: 190). A pair’s spread is `(max − min) / median` of its synced p95 across runs, and `max − min` of its two-tick share in points.

| Spread                 | Pairs | Median |  p90 | Worst                         |
|------------------------|-------|--------|------|-------------------------------|
| synced p95, relative   |   190 |     8% |  31% | 76% 960x540-busy-video GL 1   |
| two-tick share, points |   190 |    3.2 | 11.6 | 40.4 1024x768n-crt-light GL 1 |

Run-to-run drift (a whole run moving on its own: the median over scenarios of one run against another of the same version, largest of 1 pair(s)): two-tick **1.8 points**, synced p95 **3%**.

GL 1 vs 2 calls a scenario’s difference only beyond the **p90** spread *and* with the two versions’ run ranges apart; the overall shift (the median of the per-scenario differences) only beyond the run-to-run **drift**. Two-tick share is the stable metric; synced p95 is the noisy one.

No comparison: the sessions hold WebGL 1 runs only.

### Noise floor — same-GL repeats

Runs: GL 1 × 2. 195 scenario × version pair(s) measured at least twice (GL 1: 195). A pair’s spread is `(max − min) / median` of its synced p95 across runs, and `max − min` of its two-tick share in points.

| Spread                 | Pairs | Median |  p90 | Worst                      |
|------------------------|-------|--------|------|----------------------------|
| synced p95, relative   |   195 |     7% |  24% | 200% standin-zone-svg GL 1 |
| two-tick share, points |   195 |    2.9 | 10.7 | 38.7 800x600n-filter GL 1  |

Run-to-run drift (a whole run moving on its own: the median over scenarios of one run against another of the same version, largest of 1 pair(s)): two-tick **2.4 points**, synced p95 **4%**.

GL 1 vs 2 calls a scenario’s difference only beyond the **p90** spread *and* with the two versions’ run ranges apart; the overall shift (the median of the per-scenario differences) only beyond the run-to-run **drift**. Two-tick share is the stable metric; synced p95 is the noisy one.

### WebGL 1 vs 2 (plan §13, review F8)

No comparison: the sessions hold WebGL 1 runs only.

## Cross-output comparison

No cross-output comparison: no build contributes two output resolutions to this campaign. Sessions of different builds are never compared in one table (the 2026-10-27 contamination law) — every build keeps its own output block above.

# rb-mvrunonc-d6o7 — TV render bench

1 payload(s), 190 of 190 scenario(s) measured, run complete; from 10.0.0.224.
Build `baeb005` · host tizen · display 1920×1080 @1 · WebGL 1 · UA Mozilla/5.0 (SMART-TV; LINUX; Tizen 5.5) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) 69.0.3497.106/5.5 TV Safari/537.36
Each row is read against the same variant at 384×216 in this session (a knob row such as `busy-stretch` against its own plain row at the same size): **Synced p95 × 384** is the `gl.finish()`-synced render p95 over the baseline’s, **Δ points** the two-tick share’s difference. The M7’s rAF jitters to a p95 of ~30 ms on its own, so the absolute frame times are context, not budgets. One run is inside the run-to-run noise — read the campaign summary for anything that compares runs.

## Decode and upload — file to GPU (plan R1-05 B4, for R3-04)

7 page(s), 8 repetition(s) each, WebGL 1; `HTMLImageElement.decode()` yes, `createImageBitmap` yes. Fetch = XHR (`file://` on the TV), decode = an image on a blob URL (`onload` + `decode()`), upload = the renderer’s `uploadTextureSource` then `gl.finish()`; every texture is released between repetitions. "First" is the first (coldest) repetition’s file → GPU total.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| standin-zone-a | 2048×1024 | png8 | 344 | 14.60 / 32.50 | 98.00 / 178.60 | 94.60 / 149.70 | 94.70 / 149.80 | 210.30 / 360.90 | 360.90 | 71.00 / 97.50 | 22.90 / 41.00 |
| common | 2048×1024 | rgba | 328 | 16.70 / 29.10 | 183.60 / 205.10 | 168.80 / 198.10 | 168.90 / 198.10 | 368.00 / 412.50 | 376.10 | 146.70 / 161.40 | 23.90 / 34.90 |

R3-04 zone-load estimate (the measured pages: `common` + the stand-in zone pages, 3 page(s) in all, each at its median):

- **cold load** (fetch + decode + upload — a lazy zone load, B3b): **665 ms**, within R3-04’s 2000 ms;
- **zone change today** (policy "all": decoded at boot, uploaded at the change): **282 ms**, within R3-04’s 2000 ms;
- stand-in PNG-8 decode ≈ 75.6 ms per megapixel — the measured pages, not a scaled stand-in.

# rb-mvs3tv2h-b949 — TV render bench

197 payload(s), 195 of 194 scenario(s) measured, run **incomplete**; from 10.0.0.224.
Build `3e6f26d+svg-e3` · host tizen · display 1920×1080 @1 · WebGL 1 · UA Mozilla/5.0 (SMART-TV; LINUX; Tizen 5.5) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) 69.0.3497.106/5.5 TV Safari/537.36
Each row is read against the same variant at 384×216 in this session (a knob row such as `busy-stretch` against its own plain row at the same size): **Synced p95 × 384** is the `gl.finish()`-synced render p95 over the baseline’s, **Δ points** the two-tick share’s difference. The M7’s rAF jitters to a p95 of ~30 ms on its own, so the absolute frame times are context, not budgets. One run is inside the run-to-run noise — read the campaign summary for anything that compares runs.

## Decode and upload — file to GPU (plan R1-05 B4, for R3-04)

12 page(s), 8 repetition(s) each, WebGL 1; `HTMLImageElement.decode()` yes, `createImageBitmap` yes. Fetch = XHR (`file://` on the TV), decode = an image on a blob URL (`onload` + `decode()`), upload = the renderer’s `uploadTextureSource` then `gl.finish()`; every texture is released between repetitions. "First" is the first (coldest) repetition’s file → GPU total.

R3-04 zone-load estimate (the measured pages: `common` + the stand-in zone pages, 3 page(s) in all, each at its median):

- **cold load** (fetch + decode + upload — a lazy zone load, B3b): **684 ms**, within R3-04’s 2000 ms;
- **zone change today** (policy "all": decoded at boot, uploaded at the change): **302 ms**, within R3-04’s 2000 ms;
- stand-in PNG-8 decode ≈ 73.6 ms per megapixel — the measured pages, not a scaled stand-in.

# rb-mvsnhea5-i96b — TV render bench

196 payload(s), 194 of 194 scenario(s) measured, run **incomplete**; from 10.0.0.224.
Build `b2e7018` · host tizen · display 1920×1080 @1 · WebGL 1 · UA Mozilla/5.0 (SMART-TV; LINUX; Tizen 5.5) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) 69.0.3497.106/5.5 TV Safari/537.36
Each row is read against the same variant at 384×216 in this session (a knob row such as `busy-stretch` against its own plain row at the same size): **Synced p95 × 384** is the `gl.finish()`-synced render p95 over the baseline’s, **Δ points** the two-tick share’s difference. The M7’s rAF jitters to a p95 of ~30 ms on its own, so the absolute frame times are context, not budgets. One run is inside the run-to-run noise — read the campaign summary for anything that compares runs.

## 384x216 (s = 1) — the baseline (v1 as it ships)

## Decode and upload — file to GPU (plan R1-05 B4, for R3-04)

7 page(s), 8 repetition(s) each, WebGL 1; `HTMLImageElement.decode()` yes, `createImageBitmap` yes. Fetch = XHR (`file://` on the TV), decode = an image on a blob URL (`onload` + `decode()`), upload = the renderer’s `uploadTextureSource` then `gl.finish()`; every texture is released between repetitions. "First" is the first (coldest) repetition’s file → GPU total.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| standin-zone-a | 2048×1024 | png8 | 344 | 13.30 / 34.90 | 103.00 / 123.00 | 94.20 / 98.70 | 94.20 / 98.70 | 216.50 / 242.50 | 242.50 | 63.30 / 83.00 | 22.90 / 25.50 |
| common | 2048×1024 | rgba | 328 | 19.10 / 51.60 | 174.50 / 272.50 | 171.60 / 192.60 | 171.60 / 192.60 | 370.30 / 497.70 | 348.30 | 144.00 / 153.60 | 23.00 / 31.90 |

R3-04 zone-load estimate (the measured pages: `common` + the stand-in zone pages, 3 page(s) in all, each at its median):

- **cold load** (fetch + decode + upload — a lazy zone load, B3b): **671 ms**, within R3-04’s 2000 ms;
- **zone change today** (policy "all": decoded at boot, uploaded at the change): **285 ms**, within R3-04’s 2000 ms;
- stand-in PNG-8 decode ≈ 74.6 ms per megapixel — the measured pages, not a scaled stand-in.

# rb-mvt2ahx6-xenx — TV render bench

197 payload(s), 195 of 194 scenario(s) measured, run **incomplete**; from 10.0.0.224.
Build `d4f9351+svg-e3` · host tizen · display 1920×1080 @1 · WebGL 1 · UA Mozilla/5.0 (SMART-TV; LINUX; Tizen 5.5) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) 69.0.3497.106/5.5 TV Safari/537.36
Each row is read against the same variant at 384×216 in this session (a knob row such as `busy-stretch` against its own plain row at the same size): **Synced p95 × 384** is the `gl.finish()`-synced render p95 over the baseline’s, **Δ points** the two-tick share’s difference. The M7’s rAF jitters to a p95 of ~30 ms on its own, so the absolute frame times are context, not budgets. One run is inside the run-to-run noise — read the campaign summary for anything that compares runs.

## Decode and upload — file to GPU (plan R1-05 B4, for R3-04)

12 page(s), 8 repetition(s) each, WebGL 1; `HTMLImageElement.decode()` yes, `createImageBitmap` yes. Fetch = XHR (`file://` on the TV), decode = an image on a blob URL (`onload` + `decode()`), upload = the renderer’s `uploadTextureSource` then `gl.finish()`; every texture is released between repetitions. "First" is the first (coldest) repetition’s file → GPU total.
